# Main-Agent Pass Slicing Ledger

This file is the durable main-agent-owned record of how meso-level Architect / Designer contracts are split into pass-level Creator, Checker, and Reviewer work.

`SYSTEM_BLUEPRINT.md` remains the active status board. This ledger records the scheduling decision, covered-micro boundary, and rationale so later main agents do not rediscover or accidentally widen previous pass slices.

## Rules

- Only the main agent updates this file.
- Record a decision before or at the same time a Creator, Checker, or Reviewer packet is dispatched.
- Keep Designer artifacts meso-scoped; do not ask Designers to pre-slice implementation passes.
- Every Creator-synced Checker pass mirrors its Creator pass exactly.
- Keep meso integration passes separate from Creator-synced Checker passes.
- When a structural cleanup pass exists, list each cleanup objective separately and record whether it is fully closed or intentionally deferred.

## Current Pass Slicing Decisions

### `cross_meso_vfs_to_bio_topology`

**Decision:** The accepted `pass_115` through `pass_118` topology/audit artifacts are now sliced for the next lock-domain refactor wave. The VFS API vertical slices remain the coverage index, but they are not implementation pass units. Implementation must be sliced by lock-domain closure and shared helper-family closure. The earlier tentative `A/B/C` split over regular-file content, parent-backed entry-set publish, and namespace mutation is rejected as independent production slicing because all three mutate the same `InodeLock` domain and share helper families. The accepted replacement is one full `InodeLock` domain contract followed by staged landings that keep the intermediate state explicit, then separate `FsLock`, `PageCache/ReentryLock`, and final cleanup passes.

**Deferred / Exit Notes:**

- The `pass_118` VFS/API vertical-slice matrix is the checklist for coverage, not the implementation pass list.
- Do not split the inode-domain conversion into independent regular-file, entry-set, and namespace production passes. Those are review subclusters inside `pass_119` / `pass_121`, not separate lock-consolidation landings.
- Intermediate states are allowed only when explicit: `pass_120` may introduce substrate while old locks remain; `pass_121` performs the domain switch; `pass_122` removes the old projection locks and wrappers. No pass may leave the same helper family serving both old and new same-domain lock boundaries without an explicit exit note.
- `FsLock` and `PageCache/ReentryLock` may be designed separately from `InodeLock`, but implementation order must respect the static hierarchy `FsLock -> InodeLock guard set -> PageCache/ReentryLock -> page-cache / BIO`.
- `pass_126` reopens only the static global topology needed to decide whether allocation becomes a formal domain and where it sits relative to `InodeLock`; it does not reopen accepted behavior or authorize production edits.
- `pass_126` alone does not authorize Designer or Creator work. `pass_127` must first turn the accepted domain order into complete VFS-to-BIO vertical static boundaries and one shared-helper intersection map, using `pass_117` and `pass_118` as the required coverage basis.
- `AllocLock / AllocGuard` is the preferred candidate. Rejecting it requires a complete per-slice proof that filesystem metadata plus allocation paths can use one `FsLock` without hidden reacquisition, unclear ownership, or unacceptable serialization.
- `lock-owning wrapper` and immutable snapshot/projection are not final cleanup dispositions. Downstream contracts must converge each wrapper to the unique public admission boundary or delete it, and must inline/delete snapshots unless a narrow cross-domain publication invariant proves one must survive.
- `pass_128` continuous-guard rule: within one atomic process, an acquired domain guard remains held until that process completes. A held write guard is also the read proof for that domain. Designer must reject read-under-write relock, snapshot/drop/reacquire, same-domain split transactions, and release/reacquire justified only by blocking or scheduling concerns.
- `pass_128` helper-disposition rule: every existing lock-facing helper in the accepted `pass_117`/`pass_127` scope must be classified as the unique public acquisition root, a guard-required shared core, a pure immutable/CPU helper, or inline/delete. Migration-only wrapper/snapshot classifications cannot survive into Creator authorization.
- `pass_128` requires serialized logical review by a Sol Architect after the Designer returns. No Creator pass may open from main-agent structural review alone.
- User execution override on July 10, 2026: the post-`pass_122` xfstests gate showed no valid filesystem failure because `generic/001` was blocked by an environment image lock after the invalid `8min` timeout attempt. The user chose to treat `pass_122` as manually ended and move directly to `pass_124` to repair the known PageCache/Reentry no-climb issue before rerunning the 52-case surface.
- Checker and Reviewer slicing is intentionally not finalized here. Each Creator landing from this wave needs a matching validation/review route decided after the accepted Designer contract names the concrete write-set and dynamic hold/drop constraints.
- User execution override for this wave: proceed through `pass_122` without changing the current pass numbers. Any temporary or repair work must be absorbed into the already sliced pass that owns it; do not insert new interim pass IDs.
- Model-control audit after the Codex subagent update: the current `spawn_agent` interface exposes no model selector and spawned threads inherit the parent thread model. Session metadata proves the `pass_128` Designer and `pass_129` Architect review both actually ran as `gpt-5.6-sol` with high reasoning despite packet text requesting Luna. Until the interface exposes verified per-agent selection, scheduler records must use the observed inherited model and must not claim Luna or GPT-5.4 execution from prompt text alone.
- User execution override for Creator passes: `pass_120`, `pass_121`, and `pass_122` Creator packets must require the Creator to run the in-container repo harness cargo-check before submitting an artifact, so the main agent does not receive a non-compiling tree.
- User validation override for inode-side acceptance: the `pass_121` gate ran the prior `53`-case surface and failed only `generic/192`; the user reclassified `generic/192` as an expected VFS-layer failure that must be ignored because this wave cannot modify that layer. From this point forward the authoritative inode-side runtime surface is the same list minus `generic/192`, i.e. `52` cases. If any non-`generic/192` case fails and the cause is not obvious, preserve/classify the receipt and keep moving toward the simpler lock state rather than spending the wave on deep debug.

### `xfstests_linux_baseline_20260624`

**Decision:** The June 24 Linux-loop exFAT baseline under `.agents/tmp/` is the current broad upstream-suite scheduling baseline for `exfat_refactor`. The unfiltered receipt `test-exfat-linux.txt` ran the generic candidate space and records 633 default `[not run]` exclusions, mostly exFAT capability exclusions such as symlink, hardlink, xattr, ACL, reflink, dedupe, fallocate, fiemap, quota, and journaling features. The filtered receipt `test-exfat-linux-run.txt` is the actionable baseline: 151 tests actually ran on Linux exfat, with 147 passes and 4 Linux-side failures (`generic/169`, `generic/528`, `generic/563`, `generic/740`). Main-agent slicing keeps the runnable Linux-exfat set in three named runlists and does not schedule broad `short.list`, `full.list`, multi-section, or overlay execution yet.

**Supplemental July 6, 2026 decision:** Accepted `pass_73` plus `pass_74` open one cross-meso destructive convergence wave for ordinary parent-entry-set ownership. Under explicit user override, the implementation is serialized into small Creator/Reviewer rounds instead of one large patch. Preparatory cleanup rounds may run exactly one Creator-owned container `cargo-check` preflight each and then stop for Reviewer judgment; the same Reviewer lane should be reused across rounds when possible. The Designer-frozen runtime gates (`generic/701` first, then `generic/694`) remain reserved for the later round that actually migrates all currently known single-entry-set directory adopters together; earlier preparatory rounds do not claim that runtime coverage.

**Harness boundary:** The upstream initramfs xfstests lane must keep TEST and SCRATCH devices distinct and must carry `XFSTESTS_SCRATCH_DEV` into generated `local.config`. The June 24 harness edit makes the static fallback `local.config` honor `XFSTESTS_TEST_DEV`, `XFSTESTS_SCRATCH_DEV`, and `XFSTESTS_MOUNT_OPTIONS`, and `run_xfstests.sh` now refuses identical TEST/SCRATCH device paths before invoking upstream `./check`.

### `xfstests_classification_20260509`

**Decision:** `S1A` remains the named-xfstests classification wave for the current direct-boot path. The accepted no-scratch decision pass already proved the batch can start without `SCRATCH_DEV`, and the autosync-corrected receipt exported real testcase failures instead of the old duplicate-`DentryKey` crash. The filtered rerun over `generic/001`, `generic/007`, and `generic/013` replaced that old exported-failure surface with a non-exporting `/tmp/r`-phase `HANG / STALE STATE`. The observability-enabled rerun proved that EXIT-trap-only files are still insufficient because they never become host-visible when the run does not return, and the console-streaming rerun narrowed the stale point to visible `generic/001` filesystem-consistency output followed by a flat `check_stdout_bytes=233`. The next pass leaves the xfstests batch aside and reuses that same preserved test image in a fresh NixOS boot for one direct guest-side `fsck -t exfat_refactor /dev/vdb` probe. `generic/023` stays out of execution because it is a symlink-capability `notrun`, not a logic failure.

### `lock_structure_repair_20260506`

**Decision:** The accepted Architect audit and Loop A Designer spec-only artifact repair the lock/state substrate across `meso_01_mount_volume_state`, `meso_02_free_space_accounting_and_discard`, `meso_05_file_content_mapping_and_cached_io`, `meso_06_file_content_mutation`, and `meso_07_file_sync_and_persistence`. Under the May 6, 2026 user override, this wave uses Creator-owned `cargo-check` plus direct main-agent Reviewer, with no Checker, no ktest artifact, and no functional-test plan. Loop A is sliced into two serialized Creator cleanup passes so `fs.rs` / `bitmap.rs` mount-allocator state and `inode/*` inode-local state can be reviewed independently. Use the same live Creator for A2 after A1 is accepted when practical, but do not execute A2 before A1 lands.

| Pass ID | Kind | Covered Boundary | Slicing Rationale | Decision State |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |

Low-risk identity `.map_err(Error::from)` cleanup may be included only where naturally local to `fs.rs` / `bitmap.rs`. | `ExfatFs` mount state and allocator authority are tightly coupled by mount publication, superblock/statfs, sync posture, and free-space truth, but they should land before inode-local state consolidation so later inode changes can consume stable mount/allocator names without also rewriting owner placement. | Accepted by direct main-agent Reviewer. Creator artifact `.agents/components/lock_structure_repair_20260506/pass_a1_lock_structure_repair_20260506_mount_allocator_state_creator.md`; cargo-check receipt `.agents/checker-runs/lock_structure_repair_20260506/20260506-103623-lock_structure_repair_20260506-pass_a1_mount_allocator_state_creator/summary.tsv`. **Cleanup Objective Closure:**

- **Line A:** Owner/helper structural cleanup closed by promoting `AllocationBitmapRecord` to `AllocationBitmap`, moving bitmap helper behavior under that owner boundary, and removing helper-only `LogicalClusterRange`.
- **Line B:** Return-carrier and temporary error-seam cleanup closed by using a pass-local `FreeSpaceAccountingError` across the accounting/reporting/recount lane and making the mount/bootstrap conversion seam explicit.

**Deferred / Exit Notes:**

- Pass02 must commit free-space correctness before any discard hint and must downgrade / disable future discard attempts without rolling back bitmap truth or cached counters.
- Pass02 is also the latest acceptable point to confirm whether `FreeSpaceAccountingError` is promoted from pass-local boundary to the stable meso-owned error surface for widened discard behavior.
- Pass03 must keep administrative `FITRIM` distinct from online discard. Under current Asterinas interfaces, an explicit `EOPNOTSUPP` boundary is a valid implementation result; it must not silently pretend real trim I/O exists.
- Pass03 may define meso-local operation/outcome carriers for administrative trim admission, but carrier-specific ioctl routing, privilege checks, and user ABI remain outside this pass unless the main agent later schedules `meso_11` coordination.
- Neither pass02 nor pass03 may reuse `MountVolumeStateError` as a broad shared enum. Any temporary seam needs an explicit exit plan before the pass can reach Reviewer.
- Structural cleanup after pass02 or pass03 is not pre-sliced. If Reviewer finds helper-family, owner-placement, return-carrier, or error-seam debt, the main agent must open a named cleanup pass with individuated objectives before continuing toward integration.
- User-directed full-surface structural audits are separate from ordinary cleanup packets: they exist to force explicit disposition of every surviving helper / `struct` in the named write-set when generic cleanup wording proved too weak.

### `meso_03_directory_lookup_and_identity`

**Decision:** Slice the meso contract into one durable-structure-backed lookup Creator pass, one durable-structure-backed directory enumeration Creator pass, one independent meso integration Checker pass, and one post-acceptance lock-boundary cleanup pass. The slicing vocabulary may cite inherited Asterinas VFS directory surfaces, but it must not create or freeze new Rust signatures, request / outcome carrier names, or dispatcher families.

| Pass ID | Kind | Covered Boundary | Slicing Rationale | Decision State |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `pass_01_directory_lookup_and_identity_lookup_resolution` | Creator-Synced Pass | Lookup resolution grounded in two durable inputs: the validated Up-case Table On-disk Structure Owner published by `meso_01_mount_volume_state`, and this directory's checksum-validated `DirectoryEntrySet` material. Covers mounted codec selection, Up-case folding, `NameHash` as prefilter only, trailing-dot-sensitive folded-name comparison under `keep_last_dots`, contiguous primary / secondary entry-set validation, location-derived child identity from the matched entry-set location, alias-equivalent spelling coherence, create-oriented stale-negative revalidation, and separation among absence, invalid caller spelling, and integrity / I/O failure on the inherited lookup surface. | The first implementation pass should make ordinary lookup correct as one coherent durable-structure pipeline. Splitting Up-case Table consumption, DirectoryEntrySet validation, location-derived identity, alias coherence, and stale-negative revalidation into separate early passes would either freeze artificial helper seams or leave a Checker with only partial, non-user-meaningful lookup behavior. | Checker rerun 02 PASS confirmed. Reviewer found a structural `iocharset` lookup seam in `inode.rs`, but user explicitly decided to absorb that seam cleanup into `pass_02_directory_lookup_and_identity_readdir_visibility_boundary` and treat `pass_01` as accepted. |
| `pass_02_directory_lookup_and_identity_readdir_visibility_boundary` | Creator-Synced Pass | Directory enumeration grounded in the same checksum-validated `DirectoryEntrySet` material and the same location-derived identity rules from `pass_01`: scan-order traversal, traversal-progress semantics on the inherited enumeration surface, inherited visitor rejection / stop behavior, visible-entry filtering, dirent identity emission from durable entry-set location, and typed benign-versus-critical handling for unrecognized directory entries on the enumeration-facing surface. User-carried forward cleanup also absorbs the unresolved lookup-side `iocharset` seam from `pass_01`: either the published codec policy shapes a real lookup/enumeration codec boundary here, or the seam is narrowed / documented precisely enough that it no longer survives as an inert no-op publication consumer. | Enumeration has a distinct public behavior boundary: offset/progress handling, visitor failure propagation, visible-entry filtering, and unrecognized-entry presentation. Keeping it separate lets `pass_01` stabilize child resolution while `pass_02` explicitly proves enumeration reuses the same durable DirectoryEntrySet and Up-case Table / identity truths rather than inventing a parallel namespace path. The user-directed carry-forward avoids reopening accepted `pass_01` solely for the inert codec seam. | Planned; absorb the carried `iocharset` seam cleanup before or with `pass_02` dispatch. |
| `pass_03_directory_lookup_and_identity_integration` | Meso Integration Checker Pass | Cross-surface validation that inherited lookup and enumeration surfaces present one coherent namespace view over the same Up-case Table On-disk Structure Owner and DirectoryEntrySet truth: mixed-case and trailing-dot-sensitive lookup, alias-equivalent identity reuse, repeated-call stability, stale-negative revalidation, and integrity-failure separation for fractured / unrecognized entry sets. | The Designer ktest couples lookup identity, enumeration visibility, and anomaly classification across both inherited VFS surfaces, so the final acceptance proof must remain Checker-owned and separate from the two Creator-synced passes. | Planned; dispatch only after `pass_01` and `pass_02` are accepted. |
| `pass_04_directory_lookup_and_identity_lock_boundary_cleanup_01` | Creator Cleanup Pass. Covered micro-features: `Directory-entry sets must be read as contiguous checksum-validated units`; `Stable inode identity comes from directory-entry location`; `Create-oriented resolution cannot trust stale negative-cache state`; `Name matching is encoding-aware, case-folded, and trailing-dot-sensitive`; `Unrecognized directory entries impose typed lookup-facing boundaries` | This post-acceptance cleanup lane repairs the remaining read-side lock-boundary mismatch between the accepted `meso_03` Designer contract and the current implementation. It may narrow or reorganize owner-local directory read helpers so `lookup()` / `readdir_at()` stop relying on transient lockless directory-stream facts across blocking I/O, revalidate facts when required by the accepted Designer contract, and remain disjoint from the regular-file stream-extent lock contract owned by later file-content meso work. | Opened by user priority shift; archive the Creator packet now and hold dispatch sequencing separate from the paused `meso_05` wave. |

**Deferred / Exit Notes:**

- `pass_01` intentionally carries the lookup-facing identity and stale-negative obligations together with Up-case Table consumption and DirectoryEntrySet validation; do not peel alias reuse or create-oriented revalidation into a third early Creator pass unless a future Reviewer rejection proves the current slice is too broad.
- `pass_02` must reuse the durable naming / identity rules already established by `pass_01`; it must not invent a separate enumeration-only identity path, weaker DirectoryEntrySet validation, or weaker unrecognized-entry policy.
- Mentions of inherited VFS names such as lookup, enumeration, or visitor behavior are routing anchors only. Creator packets must not treat this slicing ledger as authorization to invent new aggregate request / outcome carriers, new dispatcher enums, or exact function signatures beyond pre-existing stable kernel interfaces.
- Structural cleanup is not pre-sliced. If Reviewer later finds helper-family, identity-cache, return-carrier, or test-topology debt, open a named cleanup pass with explicit objectives instead of widening `pass_01` or `pass_02` after the fact.
- `pass_04_directory_lookup_and_identity_lock_boundary_cleanup_01` is a contract-alignment cleanup only. It may repair read-side lock/revalidation behavior and adjacent owner-local helper structure, but it must not absorb regular-file mapping locks or reopen directory semantics that belong to accepted `meso_03` behavior passes except where needed to preserve the same externally visible results under the corrected inlet rules.

### `meso_04_directory_entry_mutation`

**Decision:** Architect and Designer artifacts are accepted for the baseline `meso_04` surface, and the follow-up architecture repair for single-entry / published-entry-set field mutation is accepted. This meso now includes one planned post-integration substrate pass for validated field republish and slot-aligned cleanup support, plus one new post-acceptance lock-boundary cleanup pass, in addition to the earlier owner-local skeleton and user-visible create / remove / rename passes.

| Candidate Pass Area | Current Decision | Rationale |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `pass_01_directory_entry_mutation_direntry_owner_local_skeleton` | Creator Cleanup Pass. Covered micro-features: `File and directory names live in consecutive directory-entry sets guarded by set checksums`; `directory removal and directory-target rename require an emptiness gate`; `Unrecognized directory entries impose typed invalidity and no-modify boundaries instead of generic ignore behavior` | `meso_03` established low-level read-side helpers, but it did not leave behind one shared owner-local directory-entry mutation substrate. Without an explicit first pass, `create`, `unlink` / `rmdir`, and `rename` would each re-open scan / classification / emptiness / slot-location logic inside long `inode.rs` methods. This pass may introduce only an owner-local internal seam for validated entry-set walking, slot-range / vacancy discovery, emptiness scanning, and typed invalidity classification under `ExfatInode(dir)`; it must not invent a new public facade, request / outcome carrier family, or Designer-frozen generic interface layer. |
| `pass_02_directory_entry_mutation_create_mkdir_publication` | Creator-Synced Pass. Covered micro-features: `File and directory names live in consecutive directory-entry sets guarded by set checksums`; `create and mkdir secure directory slots before committing new entry sets`; `zero_size_dir changes only the newborn directory's initial allocation shape`; `Asterinas path-tree surface is positional and still requires explicit refusal boundaries` | After `pass_01` establishes the shared owner-local direntry substrate, creation becomes the first coherent user-visible publication boundary: parent slot reservation, optional parent growth, contiguous checksum-valid entry-set publication, and newborn-directory shape rules can be implemented without entangling delete or rename ordering. This pass should also close the local refusal boundary for unsupported adjacent tree-mutation surfaces that are naturally exercised near the inherited create-side routing. |
| `pass_03_directory_entry_mutation_unlink_rmdir_ordering` | Creator-Synced Pass. Covered micro-features: `unlink and rmdir invalidate entry sets before freeing their cluster state`; `directory removal and directory-target rename require an emptiness gate`; `Unrecognized directory entries impose typed invalidity and no-modify boundaries instead of generic ignore behavior` | Delete-like paths share one distinct correctness contract: namespace-first invalidation, live emptiness gating for directory removal, typed refusal on invalid / unrecognized target material, and allocator handoff that never leaves a visible name pointing at reclaimed clusters. Keeping them together gives Checker one clear failure-ordering proof. |
| `pass_04_directory_entry_mutation_rename_ordering` | Creator-Synced Pass. Covered micro-features: `Cross-directory rename secures the new home before invalidating the old one`; `rename accepts ordinary semantics and RENAME_NOREPLACE only`; `Cross-directory rename crash windows may transiently duplicate reachability rather than orphan it`; `directory removal and directory-target rename require an emptiness gate`; `Asterinas path-tree surface is positional and still requires explicit refusal boundaries` | Rename is the highest-coupling mutation path: it spans same-directory and cross-directory rewriting, destination-first ordering, durable-identity lock order, the allowed rename semantic boundary, and directory-target emptiness. It should not be mixed with creation or delete-like cleanup because those would blur the crash-window and multi-directory admission proof. |
| `pass_05_directory_entry_mutation_integration` | Meso Integration Checker Pass | Integration remains Checker-owned and waits for accepted implementation passes. It should validate the full create → cross-directory rename → unlink / rmdir namespace sequence plus failure-maintenance scenarios after `pass_02` through `pass_04` are closed. |
| `pass_06_directory_entry_mutation_entry_set_field_update_substrate` | Creator-Synced Pass. Covered micro-features: `Validated published file-entry-set field updates must republish one checksum-valid set`; `File and directory names live in consecutive directory-entry sets guarded by set checksums`; `unlink and rmdir invalidate entry sets before freeing their cluster state`; `Unrecognized directory entries impose typed invalidity and no-modify boundaries instead of generic ignore behavior` | This follow-up pass owns the reusable write-side substrate that later metadata meso components need: admit one already-validated published file entry set as mutable material, share the existing decode / validation invariants with the immutable read path instead of cloning a second parser, republish recognized-field byte updates with a refreshed checksum, and keep slot-aligned invalidation / publication cleanup available as a narrower writable-span path that does not itself prove ordinary mutability. Keeping it separate avoids retroactively widening the earlier user-visible namespace passes while still closing the capability gap those later metadata waves would otherwise have to reopen themselves. |
| `pass_07_directory_entry_mutation_lock_boundary_cleanup_01` | Creator Cleanup Pass. Covered micro-features: `File and directory names live in consecutive directory-entry sets guarded by set checksums`; `create and mkdir secure directory slots before committing new entry sets`; `unlink and rmdir invalidate entry sets before freeing their cluster state`; `Cross-directory rename secures the new home before invalidating the old one`; `rename accepts ordinary semantics and RENAME_NOREPLACE only`; `Cross-directory rename crash windows may transiently duplicate reachability rather than orphan it`; `directory removal and directory-target rename require an emptiness gate`; `Asterinas path-tree surface is positional and still requires explicit refusal boundaries`; `Unrecognized directory entries impose typed invalidity and no-modify boundaries instead of generic ignore behavior` | This post-acceptance cleanup lane repairs the remaining mutation-side lock-boundary mismatch between the accepted `meso_04` Designer contract and the current implementation. It should align the mutating inlet and shared serialization path across `create`, `unlink`, `rmdir`, and `rename`, close the uncovered `unlink()` admission path, preserve durable-identity ordering for cross-directory rename, and keep allocator entry subordinate to already-settled namespace admission / emptiness / collision decisions without widening the meso into new semantics. |

**Deferred / Exit Notes:**

- `pass_01` is not a license to invent a reusable public `direntry` API. The intended target is an owner-local internal substrate under `ExfatInode(dir)` and adjacent exFAT-local code only.
- `pass_01` may create a dedicated `direntry`-focused Rust file only if the Creator can justify that the seam is still owner-local / exFAT-local and materially reduces duplicated scan logic without introducing a new carrier family.
- Later passes must reuse the `pass_01` substrate instead of cloning directory-entry walking, emptiness scanning, or typed invalidity classification loops into separate long methods.
- `pass_02` through `pass_04` remain user-visible behavior passes. If `pass_01` grows beyond the internal direntry skeleton boundary, reject it and route a narrower repair instead of silently absorbing public mutation semantics early.
- `pass_07_directory_entry_mutation_lock_boundary_cleanup_01` is a lock-contract alignment cleanup only. It may tighten the shared mutating inlet, close uncovered mutation entry points, and narrow ad hoc serialization seams, but it must preserve the already accepted namespace ordering and refusal semantics rather than re-slicing create / delete / rename behavior into a new public contract.
- `pass_06` must preserve the distinction between two write-side roles: validated published entry-set field republish versus writable slot-span cleanup / invalidation. The latter is a narrower byte-authoring capability and must not become proof that arbitrary directory bytes are ordinarily mutable.
- `pass_06` should maximize code sharing with the existing read-side validation path. If the implementation starts cloning file-entry-set parse / checksum / recognized-field classification logic into a parallel mutable stack, reject it and reopen the design rather than accepting two near-duplicate direntry pipelines.

### `meso_05_file_content_mapping_and_cached_io`

**Decision:** Architect and Designer artifacts are accepted. `pass_01` through `pass_04` remain accepted / closed as the baseline read-side implementation lanes, but the accepted `lock_structure_repair_01` wave now owns any further shared lock-substrate implementation before the reserved `pass_05` integration lane may reopen.

| Candidate Pass Area | Current Decision | Rationale |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `pass_01_file_content_mapping_and_cached_io_shared_read_path_and_lock_boundary` | Creator-Synced Pass. Covered micro-features: `Ordinary cached I/O uses generic cached-data paths with exFAT-specific boundary guards`; `Block mapping and size mutation require a synchronization boundary against truncate` | This first lane is user-fixed. It closes the shared read-path factoring and lock-boundary repair needed before any user-visible regular-file mapping surface can safely build on it. The pass may touch owner-local shared stream/backing translation code and the currently adjacent directory read helper / lock choreography only as a prerequisite repair to avoid duplicating `NoFatChain` / FAT-chain traversal and to remove the current directory/file lock inconsistency. It must not widen into new directory semantics, namespace behavior, or regular-file mutation publication. |
| `pass_02_file_content_mapping_and_cached_io_byte_read_and_initialized_boundary` | Creator-Synced Pass. Covered micro-features: `Stream Extension encodes allocation topology and initialized-data extent`; `Ordinary cached I/O uses generic cached-data paths with exFAT-specific boundary guards` | Once the shared read-side substrate and lock boundary are stable, byte-read behavior is the narrowest user-visible file surface: EOF clipping, `ValidDataLength` zero-observable suffix handling, wrong-kind refusal, invalid-range rejection, and imported read-side fast-fail posture. Keeping it separate lets Checker prove initialized-data exposure without yet coupling in `bmap` or page-cache backend async segmentation. |
| `pass_03_file_content_mapping_and_cached_io_block_mapping_and_page_count` | Creator-Synced Pass. Covered micro-features: `Block mapping takes an O(1) path for contiguous NoFatChain streams`; `Stream Extension encodes allocation topology and initialized-data extent`; `Ordinary cached I/O uses generic cached-data paths with exFAT-specific boundary guards` | Logical block mapping and `npages` are one coherent read-side translation lane: both consume the shared stream-shape truth, both must respect EOF / initialized-data boundaries, and both are narrower than full page-cache backend BIO translation. Keeping them together gives one focused proof that contiguous arithmetic mapping and FAT-chain traversal agree with file length / page-count reporting. |
| `pass_04_file_content_mapping_and_cached_io_page_cache_backend_translation` | Creator-Synced Pass. Covered micro-features: `PageCacheBackend must translate page indices into possibly fragmented disk I/O without blocking inline`; `Stream Extension encodes allocation topology and initialized-data extent`; `Ordinary cached I/O uses generic cached-data paths with exFAT-specific boundary guards`; `Block mapping and size mutation require a synchronization boundary against truncate` | Backend page translation is the highest-coupling read-side path: it depends on the shared lock boundary, fragmented backing segmentation, initialized-data zero handling, waiter handoff, and non-blocking rules under page-cache call sites. It is therefore intentionally last among implementation passes, after the lower-level byte-read and block-mapping surfaces have stabilized. |
| `pass_05_file_content_mapping_and_cached_io_integration` | Meso Integration Checker Pass (reserved / blocked behind `lock_structure_repair_01`) | Integration remains Checker-owned, but it must stay closed until the routed `lock_structure_repair_01` passes re-establish topology conformance for the surviving read-side implementation. Only then should it validate agreement across `read_at`, logical block mapping, `npages`, cached page read translation, cached dirty-page writeback translation, repeated-call stability, failure-maintenance, and the shared truncate anti-race boundary. |

### `lock_structure_repair_01`

**Decision:** Execute the accepted cross-wave lock-repair plan through four routed implementation passes: one shared substrate setup pass plus three parent-meso conformance cleanup passes, followed by one user-directed wave-level integration Checker lane and one user-directed wave-level architectural Reviewer lane. Because Creator / Checker / Reviewer packets still require exactly one parent meso-component, `lock_structure_repair_01` remains the canonical wave name while routed implementation / validation packets bookkeep under `meso_03_directory_lookup_and_identity`, `meso_04_directory_entry_mutation`, and `meso_05_file_content_mapping_and_cached_io`.

| Candidate Pass Area | Current Decision | Rationale |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `pass_01_lock_structure_repair_01_shared_substrate` | Creator Cleanup Pass. Parent meso routing: `meso_05_file_content_mapping_and_cached_io`. Covered boundary: shared live Level-1 admission substrate, true Level-2 owner-local inode admission substrate, narrowed Level-3 stream-shape responsibility, allocator-last ordering hooks, durable-identity-based multi-inode ordering substrate, and the narrow release / revalidate snapshot handoff needed so later blocking storage submission does not depend on a live spin guard. It must not claim final directory-read, directory-mutation, or regular-file mapping conformance by itself. | Isolate the highest-risk shared lock-shape edits before re-homing public entry surfaces. Route it through `meso_05` because the reopened blocker came from `meso_05/pass_04`, the recovered Architect audit was sourced from the `meso_05` sidecar, and later regular-file mapping / mutation / sync work all import the same substrate. |
| `pass_02_lock_structure_repair_01_directory_lookup_rehome` | Creator Cleanup Pass. Parent meso routing: `meso_03_directory_lookup_and_identity`. Covered boundary: `lookup()` / `readdir_at()` and shared directory-read choreography must enter through `ExfatFs state rwlock(Read)` plus directory `ExfatInode rwlock(Read)`, use release / reacquire / revalidate around blocking directory-byte reads, and stop using Level 3 as the owner-local directory-read substitute. | Directory read-side work is the narrowest consumer of the repaired substrate and validates the sleep-boundary choreography before write-side namespace mutation is retargeted. |
| `pass_03_lock_structure_repair_01_directory_mutation_rehome` | Creator Cleanup Pass. Parent meso routing: `meso_04_directory_entry_mutation`. Covered boundary: `create()`, `unlink()`, `rmdir()`, `rename()`, and their shared mutation substrate must use live Level-1 mutation admission, ordered participating directory Level-2 admission, optional Level-3 stream-shape entry only when needed, allocator-last ordering, and removal of `directory_mutation` as the structural authority. It must close lower-to-higher acquisition inversions without reopening accepted namespace or rename semantics. | This is the highest-coupling directory-side repair and should wait until the shared substrate and read-side directory admission have stabilized. |
| `pass_03_lock_structure_repair_01_directory_mutation_rehome_cleanup_01` | Creator Cleanup Pass. Parent meso routing: `meso_04_directory_entry_mutation`. Covered boundary: keep the accepted `pass_03` directory-mutation rehome shape, but close the residual Level-2 admission debt named by the wave-level architectural Reviewer: `rmdir()` and both `rename()` branches must acquire the ordered participating directory write set before source/target resolution, directory-target emptiness must admit the child directory through Level 2, and the surviving child-directory special-case stream-snapshot seam must disappear. | `pass_06_lock_structure_repair_01_architectural_reviewer` rejected for three specific residuals confined to the meso_04 mutation surface. Route them back into a narrow Creator cleanup pass instead of reopening the repaired regular-file or test-topology surfaces. |
| `pass_04_lock_structure_repair_01_regular_file_mapping_rehome` | Creator Cleanup Pass. Parent meso routing: `meso_05_file_content_mapping_and_cached_io`. Covered boundary: regular-file `read_at()`, block mapping, `npages()`, `read_page_async()`, `write_page_async()`, and adjacent cached-I/O translation must move onto Level-2 file-owner admission plus Level-3 stream-shape exclusion, with mapping snapshots and submission tokens surviving after the narrow lock window ends so no spin-based guard crosses block-device read, BIO submission, or wait boundaries. | This closes the surviving `meso_05` structural mismatch only after the shared substrate and directory-side conformance are in place, leaving the future `meso_06` / `meso_07` lock story compatible with the frozen topology. |
| `pass_05_lock_structure_repair_01_integration_checker` | Meso Integration Checker Pass. User-directed wave gate routed under `meso_05_file_content_mapping_and_cached_io`. Covered boundary: run only the related integration ktests for the completed `lock_structure_repair_01` wave — rerun the existing `meso_03` and `meso_04` integration exact-name ktests, and implement/run the missing `meso_05` integration exact-name ktests for read / mapping / page-cache coherence, failure-maintenance, repeated-call stability, and truncate-boundary serialization. Do not run unit ktests here. | The user explicitly replaced the normal per-pass runtime flow with one post-wave integration Checker lane. Routing bookkeeping under `meso_05` keeps the execution receipts grouped with the final read-side parent meso while still validating the full wave's affected directory and regular-file integration surfaces. |
| `pass_05_lock_structure_repair_01_integration_checker_rerun_01` | Meso Integration Checker Pass. User-directed wave gate rerun routed under `meso_05_file_content_mapping_and_cached_io`. Covered boundary: rerun the exact same integration-ktest surface as `pass_05_lock_structure_repair_01_integration_checker` after `pass_03_lock_structure_repair_01_directory_mutation_rehome_cleanup_01` lands, because the wave-level runtime evidence becomes stale once the directory-mutation production surface changes again. Do not add unit ktests here. | The reviewer rejection is structural rather than test-topology-only, so the accepted `pass_05` receipts no longer prove the post-cleanup state. The wave must refresh its single user-directed runtime gate before Reviewer follow-up. |
| `pass_06_lock_structure_repair_01_architectural_reviewer` | Reviewer Pass. User-directed wave-level architectural gate routed under `meso_05_file_content_mapping_and_cached_io` for bookkeeping while artifacts remain under `lock_structure_repair_01`. Covered boundary: judge whether the surviving global lock structure now conforms to `macro_00_global_topology.md` plus the accepted `lock_structure_repair_01` architect/designer anchors after the wave-level integration Checker passes, and re-audit the packet-touched test-support surface from `pass_05`. | This is the user-directed final structural gate for the lock-repair wave before any focused `meso_06` / `meso_07` Designer repair packet may open. |
| `pass_06_lock_structure_repair_01_architectural_reviewer_followup` | Reviewer Pass. User-directed wave-level architectural follow-up gate routed under `meso_05_file_content_mapping_and_cached_io` for bookkeeping while artifacts remain under `lock_structure_repair_01`. Covered boundary: re-judge the same global lock-structure and touched test-support surface after `pass_03_lock_structure_repair_01_directory_mutation_rehome_cleanup_01` and `pass_05_lock_structure_repair_01_integration_checker_rerun_01` land. | `pass_06_lock_structure_repair_01_architectural_reviewer` rejected the pre-cleanup state for three residual meso_04 admission defects. The same final gate must re-run after the cleanup and runtime rerun close those residuals. |

**Deferred / Exit Notes:**

- User-directed wave-local execution override for `lock_structure_repair_01`: every routed pass must dispatch a Creator, but no per-pass Reviewer packet or per-pass runtime Checker packet should be scheduled.
- Per-pass validation for this wave is limited to at most one small `cargo check` preflight after the Creator lands; the main agent may run that preflight directly via `.agents/tools/checker_run.sh cargo-check --component <parent-meso> --phase <pass-id>` or skip it when no compile-affecting delta lands, so a dedicated Checker packet is optional rather than mandatory.
- After all routed `lock_structure_repair_01` passes close, schedule exactly one Checker-owned integration lane covering only the related integration ktests for this wave; do not schedule unit ktests for the intermediate passes or the final integration gate.
- After that integration lane passes, schedule exactly one architectural Reviewer lane to judge whether the surviving global lock structure now conforms to the accepted `macro_00` topology and the `lock_structure_repair_01` architect/designer anchors.
- If that final architectural Reviewer rejects for structural cleanup and the route returns to Creator, rerun the same single wave-level integration gate before the Reviewer follow-up; the previously accepted runtime receipts are stale once production lock structure changes again.
- Do not open a standalone cross-wave Creator packet that lacks a parent meso-component; route packets through the parent mesos listed above while keeping the `lock_structure_repair_01` wave name in the pass IDs and packet / artifact naming.
- Do not reopen accepted Designer artifacts for `meso_03_directory_lookup_and_identity`, `meso_04_directory_entry_mutation`, or `meso_05_file_content_mapping_and_cached_io`; these routed passes are substrate-conformance work only.
- After `pass_05_lock_structure_repair_01_integration_checker` plus any required rerun and `pass_06_lock_structure_repair_01_architectural_reviewer` plus any required follow-up both pass, dispatch the focused `meso_06_file_content_mutation` Designer repair first, then the focused `meso_07_file_sync_and_persistence` Designer repair.
- Keep `meso_05_file_content_mapping_and_cached_io` integration reserved and keep all `meso_06` / `meso_07` Creator slicing closed until the routed `lock_structure_repair_01` passes and the later two Designer repair packets are accepted.

### `meso_06_file_content_mutation`

**Decision:** The focused post-`lock_structure_repair_01` Designer repair is accepted, `pass_01_file_content_mutation_internal_helper_boundary_and_backend_dereentry` is accepted/closed as the seam substrate, and the later `meso_06` work is now intentionally sliced into two additional Creator passes plus one later meso integration pass. Do not over-split the remaining visible mutation work: keep the public write/refusal boundary separate from the topology-bearing growth/shrink pass, then reserve integration for after both implementation passes stabilize.

| Pass ID | Kind | Covered Boundary | Slicing Rationale | Decision State |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `pass_01_file_content_mutation_internal_helper_boundary_and_backend_dereentry` | Creator-Synced Pass | Regular-file owner-internal admitted-state helper seam for the packeted `inode.rs` data path; backend-facing `page_cache` read / write / `npages` / initialization routing that must consume published facts plus already chosen file-local state without public-admission reentry; simplification or removal of regular-file helpers, thin forwarding wrappers, and snapshot-as-substitute routing whose only justification was the missing admitted-state seam; closure of the currently known child symptoms of that parent problem inside the regular-file data-path scope. This pass does **not** yet claim final user-visible write / append / resize publication, allocation growth, or sync-intent completion semantics. | Partial-page cached writes can force cache refill from inside an outer mutation path. If visible mutation work lands before this seam is repaired, the implementation either deadlocks or preserves the same architecture defect by bypassing generic `page_cache`, forbidding refill shapes, or carrying snapshot/workaround debt forward. The first pass must therefore repair the parent structure directly and collapse the known symptom surface instead of routing around it. | Accepted / closed through Creator, user-directed Creator repair 01, synchronized Checker, narrow Checker rerun 01, and Reviewer. |
| `pass_02_file_content_mutation_write_boundary_and_refusal_paths` | Creator-Synced Pass | Public regular-file `write_at` / append-intent mutation inlet on top of the accepted pass01 seam, including write-side zero-fill and initialized-data publication **when no new allocation/free topology needs to be introduced**, content-change timestamp triggering for those successful writes, `O_DIRECT` alignment rejection before side effects, explicit fallocate-family refusal, and recording/routing of `O_SYNC` / `O_DSYNC` persistence intent without absorbing `meso_07` file-sync semantics. Keep this pass out of EOF-extending allocation topology, shrink/free, `NoFatChain` transition, and orphan-vs-visible append failure ordering. | After pass01, the next safest slice is to open the public mutation boundary and the refusal/flag surfaces without simultaneously landing allocator/FAT topology rewrites. This gives later growth/shrink work a stable public inlet and keeps direct-I/O / fallocate / sync-intent boundary code from being buried inside the topology-heavy pass. | Accepted / closed through Creator, synchronized Checker, Reviewer, and two ultra-small main-agent production micro-fixes during the Checker lane. |
| `pass_03_file_content_mutation_growth_shrink_and_allocation_topology` | Creator-Synced Pass | The remaining topology-bearing visible mutation work: EOF-extending writes and append publication, resize growth and shrink, Stream Extension truth maintenance for `DataLength` / `ValidDataLength` / `FirstCluster` / `NoFatChain`, allocator/Allocation Bitmap/FAT coordination, contiguous-to-fragmented transition with FAT backfill, chain shortening and cluster release, append crash-window ordering that prefers orphanable allocation over exposed uninitialized EOF, and the length-side timestamp/publication consequences of those mutations. This pass also owns the remaining zero-fill and truncate anti-race aspects that require real size/topology change rather than the boundary-only write path from pass02. | Growth, shrink, `NoFatChain` transition, allocator/FAT publication, and append failure-maintenance form one indivisible stream/allocator story. Splitting them further would create artificial half-topology passes with ambiguous Checker obligations and would re-open the same publication-order risks across multiple routes. | Accepted / closed through Creator, synchronized Checker, and Reviewer; final Checker skippable per Reviewer verdict. |
| `pass_04_file_content_mutation_integration` | Meso Integration Pass | Meso-level success/failure/repeated-call/concurrency scenarios after pass02 and pass03 are both accepted: ordinary writes, append-intent writes, gap writes, non-contiguous growth, shrink, readback-visible zero-fill, publication/order maintenance, and shared stream/allocator invariants under concurrent read/mapping observation. Keep explicit fallocate refusal and direct-I/O alignment as Creator-synced/unit-checker proof surfaces unless later implementation evidence shows they need extra meso coupling. | The Designer contract’s integration story only becomes meaningful after the public mutation boundary and the topology-bearing mutation pass both exist. Keep integration last so the Checker validates one coherent visible mutation surface rather than replaying unstable partial substrates. | Accepted / closed through the dedicated Checker integration lane with `make-kernel` plus four exact-name meso integration ktests and archived serial-log proof. |

**Deferred / Exit Notes:**

- `pass_01_file_content_mutation_internal_helper_boundary_and_backend_dereentry` is required to solve the parent helper-layer gap directly. Do not reinterpret the pass as “implement writes first but avoid generic `page_cache`.”
- The pass boundary explicitly includes cleanup of known regular-file helper/snapshot symptoms caused by the missing internal seam, but it does **not** reopen already accepted directory-side `meso_03` / `meso_04` routing work or the accepted `lock_structure_repair_01` wave.
- `pass_02_file_content_mutation_write_boundary_and_refusal_paths` is intentionally the next pass. Do not drag allocator/FAT topology or shrink/free into it merely because the public write boundary becomes available there.
- `pass_03_file_content_mutation_growth_shrink_and_allocation_topology` is intentionally broad. Do not peel append growth, resize growth, shrink/free, and `NoFatChain` / FAT-backfill work into separate micro-passes unless later evidence shows the combined topology story is still too large to stabilize.
- Meso integration validation is now closed for `meso_06_file_content_mutation`.

### `meso_07_file_sync_and_persistence`

**Decision:** The focused post-`lock_structure_repair_01` Designer repair is accepted, and `meso_07` is now intentionally sliced into two Creator-synced implementation passes plus one later meso integration pass. Do not open a separate pre-wave structural cleanup lane first: if file-local dirty-window bookkeeping needs a new owner-local seam, keep that work inside the second pass rather than reopening `meso_06` or the lock-repair wave.

| Pass ID | Kind | Covered Boundary | Slicing Rationale | Decision State |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `pass_01_file_sync_and_persistence_writeback_ordering_and_admission_boundary` | Creator-Synced Pass | The first real ordinary-file `sync_all()` / `sync_data()` inlet on the repaired Level-1 plus Level-2 substrate: pre-admission fast-fail on imported anomaly posture, ordered composition of admitted file writeback before backing-device sync and final device-cache flush, and the base success boundary for `sync_data` versus `sync_all` without yet claiming the full post-wakeup revalidation or repeated-call story. This pass must keep persistence file-scoped and must not absorb stream mutation, allocator ownership, or mount-wide `FileSystem::sync()` policy. | The current production seam is still an `Ok(())` stub, so the safest first lane is to make the inherited ordinary-file sync anchors real while staying anchored on the already accepted lock substrate and `meso_06` publication handoff. Landing that base ordering first prevents later wait-boundary or metadata-scope work from being buried inside a zero-to-everything rewrite. | Accepted / closed through Creator, main-agent local writeback-tail micro-fix, Checker rerun, and Reviewer. |
| `pass_02_file_sync_and_persistence_revalidation_metadata_scope_and_failure_maintenance` | Creator-Synced Pass | The remaining full correctness story after the base sync seam exists: reacquire Level 1 then Level 2 after page-cache writeback or block-device waits, revalidate forced-shutdown / fast-fail posture, preserve the admitted dirty window across writeback or device-stage failure, guarantee that no spin-based guard survives writeback submission, BIO waits, device sync, or flush completion, and close the remaining `sync_data` versus `sync_all` metadata boundary, metadata-only dirty intervals, repeated clean-call stability, and success publication that clears only the admitted window while leaving later re-dirty work outstanding. If a new owner-local dirty-window ledger or dirty-class seam is required, it belongs here and must stay owner-local rather than becoming a pre-wave cross-component cleanup. | The remaining wait-boundary, failure-maintenance, and metadata-scope work all revolve around one shared question: how one admitted dirty window is tracked, revalidated, and cleared without falsely claiming the whole file clean. Splitting those surfaces further would likely force the same owner-local dirty-state seam to be introduced in one pass and then reopened immediately in the next. | Accepted / closed through Creator repair 01, Checker rerun 01, and Reviewer follow-up 01; final Checker is skippable because Reviewer made no `.rs` edits. |
| `pass_03_file_sync_and_persistence_integration` | Meso Integration Pass | Meso-level success/failure/repeated-call/concurrency scenarios after pass01 and pass02 are both accepted: `sync_data()` then `sync_all()` ordering on the same file, post-wait anomaly or device failure maintenance, metadata-only dirty-interval behavior, and concurrent re-dirty or forced-shutdown races while one sync request sleeps. Keep mount-wide sync ownership with `meso_08` and mutation/allocation ownership with `meso_06`. | The Designer contract’s integration story only becomes meaningful once both the base ordering seam and the combined revalidation/metadata-scope truth exist together. Keep integration last so the Checker validates one coherent visible file-sync surface rather than replaying unstable partial seams. | Accepted / closed through the dedicated Checker integration lane with `make-kernel` plus four exact-name meso integration ktests and archived serial-log proof. |

**Deferred / Exit Notes:**

- Do not reopen `lock_structure_repair_01`, `meso_05`, or `meso_06` before starting `meso_07`; the accepted Level-1 plus Level-2 substrate and the released sync-intent handoff are the frozen base assumptions for these passes.
- If `pass_02` needs new owner-local dirty-state bookkeeping to separate `sync_data` from `sync_all`, keep it confined to `ExfatInode` / `inode.rs`; do not spin it out into a standalone cleanup wave or a new cross-owner carrier family.
- Keep mount-wide anomaly clearing, `VolumeDirty` rewriting, and filesystem-wide quiesce out of `meso_07`; those remain reserved for `meso_08_filesystem_sync_and_volume_state`.

### `meso_08_filesystem_sync_and_volume_state`

**Decision:** Architect and Designer artifacts are accepted. Implementation is sliced into three Creator-synced passes plus one independent meso integration Checker pass.

| Pass ID | Kind | Covered Boundary | Slicing Rationale | Decision State |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `pass_01_filesystem_sync_and_volume_state_posture_and_admission_boundary` | Creator-Synced Pass | Mount-wide observation / mutation-admission posture under `ExfatFs`: imported `VolumeDirty`, `MediaFailure`, `ClearToZero`, read-only posture for state-changing mount-wide admission, and a coherent owner-local boundary for sibling mutators / discard-adjacent consumers. This pass must preserve anomaly visibility, must not clear or rewrite `VolumeDirty`, must not claim durable clean publication, and must not add a speculative public forced-shutdown ABI. If no accepted public forced-shutdown hook exists, Checker records that public-route coverage as not applicable rather than requiring a synthetic `EOPNOTSUPP` surface. | The current code has `admitted_lookup_state()` / `admitted_mutation_state()` and direct imported anomaly snapshots, but no coherent meso08 mount-wide posture seam. Landing this first prevents `FileSystem::sync()` from burying admission policy inside helper code and gives later dirty-bracket and shutdown work one owner-local state source. | Accepted / closed through Creator, Checker, and Reviewer. Final Checker is skippable because Reviewer made no `.rs` edits. |
| `pass_02_filesystem_sync_and_volume_state_sync_dirty_bracket_and_failure_maintenance` | Creator-Synced Pass | Whole-filesystem `FileSystem::sync()` plus the `VolumeDirty` durability bracket: state-changing admission marks or preserves dirty posture before lower-level mutation can leave mount-wide work outstanding, sync establishes the quiesce point, persists the clean/dirty volume-state transition only at the correct durability point, issues the final device barrier, revalidates after blocking I/O, and leaves dirty / degraded posture retryable on persistence or flush failure. This pass must not silently clear `MediaFailure` or `ClearToZero`, must not treat one inode-local `fsync` as whole-filesystem quiesce, and must not absorb forced-shutdown monotonicity. | `VolumeDirty` and recommended write ordering are the central durable-state risk. They need a synchronized Checker pass after the pass01 posture seam exists, but before forced-shutdown cross-cuts the same admission surfaces. Explicitly including the dirty-entry boundary avoids leaving integration tests with no way to create mount-wide outstanding persistence work. | Accepted / closed through Creator, Checker, Reviewer rejection, cleanup Creator, cleanup Checker, and cleanup Reviewer. Final Checker is skippable because cleanup Reviewer made no `.rs` edits. |
| `pass_03_filesystem_sync_and_volume_state_forced_shutdown_and_suppression` | Creator-Synced Pass | Forced-shutdown admission through an accepted internal route only, monotonic shutdown posture for the lifetime of the mount, and downstream suppression for ordinary mutation / discard-adjacent consumers. Public forced-shutdown routing remains unsupported unless a later accepted routing surface exists; this pass must not invent public admin ABI and must not let later sync / observation reopen writable posture. | Forced shutdown is cross-cutting and can distort the dirty-bracket proof if mixed into pass02. Keeping it separate lets Creator, Checker, and Reviewer focus on monotonicity, caller suppression, and no speculative public API growth. | Accepted / closed through Creator, Checker, and Reviewer. Final Checker is skippable because Reviewer made no `.rs` edits. |
| `pass_04_filesystem_sync_and_volume_state_integration` | Meso Integration Pass | End-to-end Designer Section 3 scenarios after pass01-pass03 acceptance: lower-level mutation leaves mount-wide persistence work, `FileSystem::sync()` quiesces it while preserving root / superblock state, failure leaves conservative dirty / degraded posture, repeated sync / observation is stable, and sync / observation / shutdown contention linearizes. | Integration is meaningful only after the posture seam, dirty-bracket sync semantics, and shutdown monotonicity all exist. It stays Checker-owned and must not fold new production policy into the integration lane. | Accepted / closed through the dedicated Checker integration lane and post-integration Reviewer. After the test-support file was renamed to `volume_sync_integration.rs`, a narrow rename-verification Checker reran `make-kernel` and the four exact-name integration ktests under the new module path; all passed. |

### `meso_09_file_metadata_projection_and_update`

**Decision:** Architect and Designer artifacts are accepted. Implementation is sliced into two Creator-synced passes plus one independent meso integration Checker pass. Keep this meso scoped to regular-file metadata projection/update under `ExfatInode(file)`; do not absorb directory metadata, file content/size mutation, file sync durability, or volume administrative identity.

| Pass ID | Kind | Covered Boundary | Slicing Rationale | Decision State |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `pass_01_file_metadata_projection_and_update_projection_substrate` | Creator-Synced Pass | Read-side regular-file metadata projection through the inherited inode metadata getters: `metadata()`, `mode()`, `owner()`, `group()`, `atime()`, `mtime()`, and `ctime()`. Covered micro-features: `File entry sets persist DOS-style attributes and local-time timestamp triples`; `Ownership and mode are mount-derived rather than natively persisted as POSIX metadata`; `Timestamp translation bridges UTC-facing VFS state to exFAT local-time fields with offset bytes`; `Asterinas metadata surface requires explicit owner, mode, and timestamp answers even when exFAT synthesizes them`. The pass must decode durable DOS attributes and timestamp families, synthesize uid/gid/mode/ctime from mount policy and the thin exFAT model, import sibling file-size / allocated-sector truth only for projection, and perform no durable metadata rewrite. | The read projection is the stable substrate needed by every later mutator and by integration reread checks. Landing it first avoids mixing getter correctness, checksum-valid entry-set republish, and policy-sensitive mutation failure handling in one large pass. | Accepted / closed through Creator, synchronized Checker, Reviewer, and final Checker rerun. |
| `pass_02_file_metadata_projection_and_update_policy_and_timestamp_mutation` | Creator-Synced Pass | Regular-file metadata mutation after the pass01 projection substrate exists: `set_mode()` maps only projected writability to the durable DOS `ReadOnly` attribute and republishes the same validated entry set; `set_owner()` / `set_group()` confirm or refuse the mount-derived ownership envelope without inventing exFAT owner fields; admitted `set_atime()` / `set_mtime()` rewrite only the owned exFAT timestamp family and UTC-offset / 10ms fields; `set_ctime()` updates only the live synthetic metadata-change projection and must not fabricate a native exFAT `ctime`; denied metadata updates preserve projected and durable state. Covered micro-features: all six meso09 micro-features, with mutation emphasis on `File entry sets persist DOS-style attributes and local-time timestamp triples`; `Ownership and mode are mount-derived rather than natively persisted as POSIX metadata`; `chmod only materially propagates the writable bit through the DOS ReadOnly attribute`; `Timestamp translation bridges UTC-facing VFS state to exFAT local-time fields with offset bytes`; `Timestamp mutation is also gated by mount-time allow_utime policy`; `Asterinas metadata surface requires explicit owner, mode, and timestamp answers even when exFAT synthesizes them`. The pass must preserve name/slot/secondary topology, create timestamps on unrelated updates, file size, stream topology, and allocation state; successful durable rewrites publish file-local metadata dirtiness for `meso_07_file_sync_and_persistence`. | Mode/ownership policy and timestamp mutation share the same regular-file metadata update boundary: mount-policy admission, validated entry-set republish, checksum preservation, refusal without partial durable state, and file-local metadata dirty publication. Merging them reduces scheduling overhead while keeping the read projection substrate separate enough to stabilize the later mutation Checker. | Accepted / closed through Creator, synchronized Checker, Reviewer rejection, cleanup Creator, cleanup Checker, and cleanup Reviewer follow-up. Final Checker skipped because the follow-up Reviewer edit was a line-level non-functional production comment only and no tests or Checker-owned surfaces changed. |
| `pass_02_file_metadata_projection_and_update_policy_and_timestamp_mutation_cleanup_01` | Creator Cleanup Pass | Same pass02 covered-micro boundary; structural closure only for Reviewer-rejected helper/census and mount-policy seam objectives: `FileEntrySetView::is_read_only`; `FileEntrySetView::last_accessed_fields`; `FileEntrySetView::last_modified_fields`; unresolved `allow_utime` / timezone mount-policy seam lacking code-level exit-plan comment. | Reviewer accepted the pass02 runtime behavior but rejected structural quality. Cleanup must close the named objectives individually without adding new metadata feature coverage or hiding the seam in generic wording. | Accepted / closed through Creator, Checker, and Reviewer follow-up. Final Checker skipped per protocol because Reviewer made only a comment-only production edit documenting the `set_mtime()` exit plan. |
| `pass_03_file_metadata_projection_and_update_integration` | Meso Integration Pass | End-to-end Designer Section 3 scenarios after pass01-pass02 acceptance: live and reread metadata projection agree after admitted mode/timestamp changes and file sync; failure paths preserve the last good projected and durable metadata; repeated metadata getters / no-op confirmations are stable; controlled races with sibling content mutation or file sync revalidate policy and self-entry-set state before publication. Covered micro-features: all six meso09 micro-features. | Integration only becomes meaningful after read projection and the combined metadata mutation pass both exist. It stays Checker-owned and must not fold new production behavior into the integration lane. | Accepted / closed through Checker, creator_repair_01, Checker rerun, and post-integration Reviewer. Final Checker skipped because Reviewer made no `.rs` edits. |
| `pass_03_file_metadata_projection_and_update_integration_creator_repair_01` | Creator Repair Pass | Narrow repair for the failed pass03 integration concurrency test: ensure regular-file entry-set republish applies stream-extension field updates before recalculating the checksum, preserving fresh reread validity after serialized metadata and content publication. | The failed integration proof found a cross-surface publication bug rather than a test-topology issue. Route the Checker repair batch to Creator without widening pass03 beyond the entry-set republish checksum-ordering defect. | Accepted / closed through Checker rerun and post-integration Reviewer. |

**Deferred / Exit Notes:**

- Do not reopen `meso_05_file_content_mapping_and_cached_io` or `meso_06_file_content_mutation` for size / allocated-sector projection; meso09 imports those truths but does not mutate them.
- Do not route file-scoped persistence into meso09. Successful metadata rewrites only publish file-local metadata dirtiness; durability and final device flush consequences remain with `meso_07_file_sync_and_persistence`.
- Do not add volume label / GUID / administrative identity APIs here; those belong to `meso_11_volume_admin_identity`.
- Structural cleanup is not pre-sliced. If Reviewer rejects helper placement, carrier seams, timestamp helper families, or test-support topology, open a named cleanup pass with each objective individually listed.

Do not absorb regular-file metadata from `meso_09_file_metadata_projection_and_update`, namespace ordering from `meso_04_directory_entry_mutation`, file content / sync durability from `meso_06_file_content_mutation` or `meso_07_file_sync_and_persistence`, or volume label / GUID administrative identity from `meso_11_volume_admin_identity`.

Landing it first isolates ordinary-directory self entry-set validation and root synthetic projection from checksum-preserving republish and namespace-mutation refresh work. | Accepted / closed through Creator, `creator_repair_01`, Checker rerun, and Reviewer. The repair loop confirmed this rationale: the original stall was a lock reentry through projection-backed `ino()`, fixed by adding a no-lock middle-helper boundary for ordered directory writes. | Accepted / closed through Creator, `creator_repair_01`, `creator_repair_02`, Checker rerun 02, Reviewer, test-support checksum cleanup Creator, cleanup Checker, and Reviewer follow-up. **Deferred / Exit Notes:**

- If Asterinas lacks a public Linux-shaped carrier for any administrative operation, pass01 must return a deliberate `EOPNOTSUPP`-class refusal or use an owner-local `ExfatFs` adapter. It must not pretend an ordinary inode / path API is the administrative home.
- Administrative trim may preserve the current explicit unsupported boundary until a real lower trim primitive exists. Do not implement allocator range scan, bitmap mutation, or online-discard downgrade here.
- Forced shutdown admission may use the existing mount-wide state transition surface, but post-shutdown read / write / sync / discard consequences remain owned by `meso_08_filesystem_sync_and_volume_state` and `meso_02_free_space_accounting_and_discard`.
- Structural cleanup is not pre-sliced. If Reviewer rejects helper placement, carrier seams, root administrative-entry seams, label encoding helper families, or test-support topology, open a named cleanup pass with each objective individually listed.

## Process Notes From Cleanup Gaps

- `production_code_quality_cleanup_20260428` is a user-directed post-acceptance cleanup wave over already accepted production code. It is intentionally sliced into three serialized Creator cleanup packets instead of reopening Architect / Designer gates: `pass_01_02_production_code_quality_cleanup_creator`, `pass_03_04_production_code_quality_cleanup_creator`, and `pass_05_06_production_code_quality_cleanup_creator`.
- The first packet owns Phase 1 safety / dead-code / dedup / inline cleanup and Phase 2 timestamp plus owner/group body-isomorphism cleanup. The second owns Phase 3 rewrite / NoFatChain cleanup and Phase 4 error / admin-carrier / visibility cleanup. The third owns Phase 5 `inode/` module split and Phase 6 `admin.rs` split.
- This wave uses dispatch stubs and live handoff recording from the protocol, but the user explicitly directed that the full protocol should not constrain code-structure changes needed for the cleanup plan.
- `meso_02` cleanup split happened because the original cleanup packet grouped owner/helper placement and carrier/error-boundary work together; visible progress on Line A was briefly mistaken for full cleanup closure.
- Future cleanup packets must list structural objectives individually, and Creator / Reviewer artifacts must disposition each objective independently.
- Checker run archives now live under `.agents/checker-runs/<meso-component>/...` so evidence remains grouped by parent meso-component.

## Rebase Watch Notes

- `meso_07_file_sync_and_persistence` introduced `PageCache::has_dirty_pages()` / `PageCacheManager::has_dirty_pages()` in `kernel/src/fs/vfs/page_cache.rs` as a narrow VFS page-cache dirty-range query. This was not a broad VFS redesign: it closed the pass02 repeated-clean `sync_data()` / `sync_all()` bug where a materialized but clean page cache was mistaken for pending writeback and caused flush-only device sync BIOs. During future upstream rebases, check whether upstream `PageCache` grew an equivalent dirty-range predicate, dirty-tag query, or `evict_range()` writeback-result API. If so, prefer converging to that upstream API and remove the local query. If not, keep this interface visible in review because exFAT refactor's file-sync clean-call stability depends on distinguishing "page cache exists" from "range contains dirty cached pages".
